# UI end-to-end sweep — 2026-08-05

Live sweep of the Zealova iOS app driven on a booted **iPhone 16e** simulator
(`E63C5B41-208A-4AED-AC9D-50B82B4CA6F1`), running a simulator build of `61a495d9`
— i.e. **including** the 13 fixes committed earlier today. Account:
`zealova.qa.07280102@mailinator.com` (users.id `1aa02a24-0224-4a5a-b1e5-3f24dcd60bdc`).
Every row below was seen on screen and screenshotted; data claims were checked
against Supabase and Render rather than inferred from pixels.

**229 rows** — **CRIT** 13 · **HIGH** 58 · **MED** 105 · **LOW** 53.
Of those, **130 are FIXED**, **7 REFUTED** and **7 NOT A BUG** (checked and dismissed — kept for the record).
**85 remain open** — **CRIT** 1 · **HIGH** 16 · **MED** 50 · **LOW** 18.

> **Status of this file.** Eight lanes have now been driven on the simulator:
> **Stats**, **Settings**, **Exercise Library**, **Programs**, **Nutrition**,
> **Home**, **Coach** and **You**. Every lane also reported surfaces it could not
> or would not reach; those are listed verbatim under [Coverage](#coverage). Read
> that section before treating this file as a survey of the app — **a surface that
> is not listed as reached was not looked at, which is not the same as clean.**
>
> **Not driven at all.** The **Workouts** lane — the Workout tab beyond its two
> entry-point rows, the active-workout session, set logging, the rest timer,
> workout detail, workout summary and the post-workout share flow — was never
> swept. Neither was **onboarding / sign-up / sign-in**, nor the **paywall and
> purchase** flow. This file says nothing whatsoever about those surfaces.
>
> **Deliberately not exercised anywhere.** Camera, photo-library, voice and
> barcode capture (the simulator has no camera, an empty photo library and no
> audio input); destructive or account-mutating actions on the shared QA account
> (Sign Out, Delete Account, Delete/Archive, Claim/Open a reward, Save on settings
> other lanes read); and AI-generation paths that would burn generations on that
> account.
>
> **Verification state.** The **Nutrition** lane has been through the adversarial
> verification pass. Rows whose **Status** begins **REFUTED** were re-driven or
> re-read against source and **dismissed** — they are retained so the same claim
> is not re-filed, and they are excluded from the live count above. Rows reading
> **OPEN (confirmed)** survived that pass with their evidence rewritten. The
> remaining plain **OPEN** rows (Stats, Settings, Library, Programs, Home, Coach,
> You) have **not** yet been adversarially verified, so their severities are
> provisional — the usual failure modes are "no data for this QA account"
> mistaken for a defect and a mis-aimed tap mistaken for a dead control. The one
> row marked **SUSPECT — re-verify** is a known casualty of the harness defect
> described under the table.

| 6 | CRIT | Home → STREAKS → "+ Add Habit" | The "+ Add Habit" row on Home is completely broken — every tap replaces the whole screen with the error boundary "Something went wrong here / This part of the screen failed to load. Go back and reopen it — your data is safe.", followed by raw Flutter internals shown to the user: "At least listener of the StateNotifier Instance of 'StateController<bool>' threw an exception when the notifier tried to update its state. The exceptions thrown are:" (and then nothing — the exception list renders empty). There is no way to add a habit from Home at all, and the failure is 100% reproducible, not a race. The sibling entry point is fine: STREAKS → "View all" opens the same `/habits` screen normally, because it omits the query param that triggers the crash. **And it is a hard dead end**: the error screen renders with no back button, no app bar and no bottom tab bar, and the iOS left-edge back gesture does nothing — its own advice \("Go back and reopen it"\) is impossible to follow, so the only escape is force-quitting the app. | `ui_home_63_addhabit_s.png` (full-screen error state). Device log (`xcrun simctl spawn … log show --predicate 'process == "Runner"'`): `GoRouter: INFO: pushing /habits?addHabit=true` → `If you are encountering this error, chances are you tried to modify a provider` → `#6 StateController.state= (package:riverpod/src/state_controller.dart:15:31)` → `#7 HabitsScreen.build (package:fitwiz/screens/habits/habits_screen.dart:70:49)`, then two PostHog `app_error` events. Root cause is at that exact line — `mobile/flutter/lib/screens/habits/habits_screen.dart:68-70` mutates a provider inside `build()`: `if (autoOpenAddSheet && !ref.read(_autoOpenFiredProvider)) { ref.read(_autoOpenFiredProvider.notifier).state = true; …}`. Riverpod forbids writing to a provider during the build phase, so the `autoOpenAddSheet == true` branch (reached only via `/habits?addHabit=true`, i.e. only from Home's "+ Add Habit") always throws. Not stale: `habits_screen.dart` was last touched by `3d19e539` (2026-07-31), well before the build under test. | Home tab → scroll to STREAKS → tap "+ Add Habit". | DONE (partial, in-scope mitigation) — Real crash confirmed (habits_screen.dart:68-70 mutates a provider inside build()). That file is outside lib/screens/home/ ownership, so fixed at the Home-owned call site: lib/screens/home/widgets/habits_section.dart _showAddHabitSheet now pushes /habits (same as working 'View all') instead of /habits?addHabit=true, eliminating the crash trigger entirely — auto-open-sheet UX is lost but the hard dead-end is gone. Added test/screens/home/widgets/habits_section_add_habit_test.dart; negative-tested (reverted → test failed on the exact addHabit=true assertion; restored → passes). Root-cause fix (moving the state write out of build()) still needs an edit to lib/screens/habits/habits_screen.dart by whoever owns that dir. |

At this point the state of the widget's element tree is no longer stable. To safely refer to a widget's ancestor in its dispose() method, save a reference to the ancestor by calling dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType() in the widget's did…" (the sentence is itself truncated mid-word). 100% reproducible, not a race — two consecutive attempts produced byte-identical screens. This is the only surface in the app that tells a user how many AI messages/food scans/form checks they have left, so on a metered free account the remaining-quota number is simply unreachable. | `ui_coach_32_topmenu_s.png` (the menu row) → `ui_coach_33_usage_s.png` (1st attempt) → `ui_coach_35_usage2_s.png` (2nd attempt, identical). Device log shows 6 PostHog `app_error` events at 19:44:05 for the first tap. Root cause is in the source: `mobile/flutter/lib/screens/chat/chat_screen.dart:900-904` — the ListTile does `Navigator.pop(context); … _showUsageInfoSheet(context);`, passing the **already-popped sheet's** BuildContext; `_showUsageInfoSheet` (`chat_screen_ext.dart:326-333`) then immediately calls `Theme.of(context)` and `ProviderScope.containerOf(context, listen: false)` on that deactivated element, which is exactly the framework assertion being shown. The backing data is fine and reachable — `GET /api/v1/subscriptions/1aa02a24-…/feature-limits` returns 200 OK in Render (request_id `13459d4f`, 1493 ms), so this is purely the client tearing down the context it then reads from. | Coach tab → "…" top-right → "Usage info". | DONE — chat_screen.dart _showOptionsMenuWithUsageInfo now captures Navigator.of(context).context BEFORE the builder closure shadows context, and guards reuse with .mounted before reopening Usage info (mirrors the existing _minimizeToFloatingChat pattern in the same file); test/screens/chat/chat_screen_usage_info_context_crash_test.dart added, negative-tested (sabotaging the fix reproduces the real 'deactivated widget' assertion). |
| 8 | CRIT | Coach → HISTORY (Chats) → row "⋮" → **Rename** | Renaming a conversation destroys the app. The moment the Rename dialog closes, the entire screen is replaced by the error boundary — "Something went wrong here / This part of the screen failed to load. Go back and reopen it — your data is safe." — followed by a raw Flutter framework assertion printed to the user: "`'package:flutter/src/widgets/framework.dart': Failed assertion: line 6268 pos 12: '_dependents.isEmpty': is not true.`". The error screen renders **outside a Material ancestor** (every line is drawn with the yellow double-underline debug style), has no app bar, no back button and no bottom tab bar, and every tap on it is ignored — its own instruction "Go back and reopen it" is impossible to follow. **Force-quitting the app is the only escape.** 100% reproducible across two cold launches. | `ui_coach_85_rowmenu_s.png` (the ⋮ menu: Rename / Archive / Delete) → `ui_coach_86_rename_s.png` (the Rename dialog) → `ui_coach_87_workout_s.png` (1st occurrence) and `ui_coach_90_afterrename_s.png` (2nd occurrence after a clean relaunch — byte-for-byte the same screen). `ui_coach_88_stuck_s.png` shows the screen unchanged after tapping the nav-bar area and the back-button area. **Control run isolates it to Rename**: after a fresh launch, opening the same ⋮ sheet and dismissing it without choosing anything leaves the Chats list perfectly healthy (`ui_coach_91_sheetonly_s.png`) — so the sheet is fine and the dialog is the trigger. Root cause in source: `mobile/flutter/lib/screens/chat/chat_sessions_screen.dart:326-394` — `_showRenameDialog` creates `final controller = TextEditingController(...)`, awaits `showDialog`, then calls `controller.dispose()` on the very next line while the dialog route is still running its exit animation and its `TextField` is still listening. Disposing a `ChangeNotifier` that still has dependents is precisely the `_dependents.isEmpty` assertion. Both dialog buttons hit that line, so **Save** crashes the same way as **Cancel**. The sibling `_confirmDelete` dialog (`:401-425`) owns no controller and is unaffected. | Coach tab → HISTORY → tap the ⋮ on any conversation row → Rename → Cancel. | DONE — already fixed pre-session in commit 019c5f02 (rename dialog owns its own controller via StatefulWidget lifecycle); regression test test/screens/chat/chat_sessions_rename_crash_test.dart passes, negative-tested in that commit. |

Flutter 3.44 added `SnackBar.persist`, defaulted to **`persist = persist ?? action != null`** (`packages/flutter/lib/src/material/snack_bar.dart:303`), and `ScaffoldMessengerState.build` now short-circuits the auto-dismiss timer with `if (snackBar.persist) return;` (`scaffold.dart:619-624`) — so **`duration` is ignored and any SnackBar carrying a `SnackBarAction` stays up forever** until something else clears it. This exact banner is `_showSmartPillLoggedSnack` (`lib/screens/nutrition/widgets/quick_log_pills.dart:632-651`, `duration: 4s` + `SnackBarAction('Undo')`), deliberately shown on the **root** messenger (`Navigator.of(context, rootNavigator: true).overlay`), which is why it survives sub-tab switches and full-screen pushes (`_updateScaffolds`/`_isRoot`, `scaffold.dart:231-245`, re-renders it into each newly pushed root Scaffold). Title `"Scrambled Eggs (with Butter) + Toast (Whole Wheat with Butter)"` matches the two-item `_recentTitle`/`_yesterdayTitle` branch — evidence is self-consistent, not a harness artifact. Swipe-to-dismiss failing is also code-explained: default `dismissDirection` is `DismissDirection.down` (`snack_bar.dart:800`), so the horizontal swipe in `ui_nutrition_65` could never dismiss it. **Systemic, not one screen: `grep -c SnackBarAction lib` = 42 call sites and `persist:` appears nowhere in `lib/`** — every action-bearing snackbar app-wide is now permanent. Sev HIGH held (pervasive occlusion + a stuck live `Undo` affordance, but no data loss and the write is real). Caveat: the row's "on a screen that cannot scroll (#108)" aggravator leans on #138, which is itself LIKELY REFUTED; the persistence defect stands on its own. Fix: pass `persist: false` at the chokepoint (a shared `showAppSnackBar` helper) rather than at 42 call sites. |

(1) The app calls **`GET /saved-workouts/upcoming`**, an endpoint that **does not exist** — it is swallowed by the `/{workout_id}` path param, so Postgres rejects the literal string: `invalid input syntax for type uuid: "upcoming"` → **400** for every user on every load. There is no `/upcoming` route in that file — `@router.get("/{workout_id}")` is declared at `:885` and the only upcoming route is `/scheduled/upcoming` at `:1223`. Client: `mobile/flutter/lib/screens/wrapped/weekly_wrapped_screen.dart:119-128` requests `'/saved-workouts/upcoming'`, `:128` `Future.wait([summaryFetch, upcomingFetch])`, `:164-171` catch → `_errorMessage = 'Couldn\'t load your week. Added a dedicated /upcoming route (registered above /{workout_id}) returning a bare JSON list with name/scheduled_date keys matching what weekly_wrapped_screen.dart reads (distinct shape from the sibling /scheduled/upcoming's {scheduled:[...],total_count}). | 61 | HIGH | You → Profile → Share & Brag → CARDS → "Day in Proof" | The card refuses to open and tells the user to do something he already did today. The request succeeds (200); the backend simply answers about the **wrong day**. `GET /share/day-in-proof` is called with no `date`, so the server falls back to `datetime.now(timezone.utc).date()` — which had already rolled over to **2026-08-06** while the user (America/Chicago) was still on **2026-08-05**. Every Western-hemisphere user therefore loses this card for the last 5-6 hours of their evening, exactly when a "day in proof" would be worth sharing. The same helper also windows on **UTC midnight** rather than the user's midnight even when a date *is* supplied, so evening logs land on the wrong day generally. | `ui_you_74_dayproof_s.png` (the toast). Render: `GET /api/v1/share/day-in-proof HTTP/1.1" **200 OK**` at 02:56:09Z — no error, so the client's null branch fired on `has_data: false`. I replayed the service query against Supabase for both candidate dates: **2026-08-05** → workout **1** ("Balanced Kinetic Flow", `completed_at` 2026-08-05T06:08:59Z, `is_completed=true`), food_logs **1** (`logged_at` 2026-08-05T22:05:35Z, `health_score` 6) ⇒ `has_data = True`; **2026-08-06** → workout 0, food 0, PR 0 ⇒ `has_data = False`. Wall clock at the time of the tap was 2026-08-06T02:59Z. Source: `backend/api/v1/share_ai.py:203` `date_iso = date or datetime.now(timezone.utc).date().isoformat()`; `backend/services/share_data_service.py:47-51` `_day_bounds` builds `datetime(y, m, d, tzinfo=timezone.utc)` and never reads `users.timezone` (= `America/Chicago` here); `:173` `has_data = bool(pr or grade or workout)`. Client: `mobile/flutter/lib/shareables/adapters/day_in_proof_adapter.dart:26` returns null on `has_data == false`, and `screens/share/share_hub_screen.dart:49-52` turns that into the toast. This is the documented local-day-window class (CLAUDE.md) reaching a share surface — the fix is `local_day_bounds(date, tz)`. | You tab → Profile → Share & Brag → tap "Day in Proof" (any time after ~19:00 US Central). | DONE (backend) — real, confirmed live via TestClient against real DB. Root cause: local-day-window class bug (CLAUDE.md's documented chokepoint issue) — services/share_data_service.py's _day_bounds built bare UTC-midnight windows, and api/v1/share_ai.py + api/v1/share_growth.py both defaulted date to datetime.now(UTC).date() instead of the user's local today. Fixed: threaded a REQUIRED timezone_str through every day-window fn (_day_bounds/_top_pr_for_day/_meal_grade_for_day/_top_workout_for_day/day_in_proof/on_this_day) via core.timezone_utils.local_day_bounds; both API endpoints (+ /share/insight-line's day branch) now resolve_timezone()+get_user_today(). Verified live: GET /share/day-in-proof?date=2026-08-05 for QA acct now has_data=true (workout+meal) matching the finding exactly; new bounds are 5h later than old UTC bounds (America/Chicago). Test: test_share_day_in_proof_local_day.py (11 tests incl. live-DB behavioral, neg-tested both the core _day_bounds fn and the endpoint layer). |

| 115 | MED | Nutrition → Hydration (backend `_fetch_hydration`) | Silent fallback renders a confident wrong screen when the request fails: on any exception the handler returns `{"current_ml": 0, "target_ml": 2500}`, so a failed hydration fetch is indistinguishable from a real "0 of 2500 ml" day. The inner goal lookup also swallows errors with a bare `except Exception: pass`. | `backend/api/v1/home/bootstrap.py:286-288` — `except Exception as e: logger.error(...); return {"current_ml": 0, "target_ml": 2500}`; lines 276-283 `except Exception: pass  # use default`. Same class as the previously-found "screen renders confidently while its request failed". Corroborated by `ui_nutrition_04_water_s.png` showing a fully-populated card for an account with zero hydration rows. | N/A — code path; triggers whenever the hydration query or `user_settings` lookup raises. | **REFUTED** — the fallback dict is byte-identical to `HydrationSummary`'s own Pydantic defaults (`bootstrap.py:126-129` `current_ml: int = 0`, `target_ml: int = 2500`) and line 505 maps a falsy dict to `HydrationSummary()`, so deleting 286-288 emits the exact same payload — the cited lines are not the fabrication source. The screenshot is not corroboration: `ui_nutrition_04_water_s.png` is `screens/nutrition/tabs/hydration_tab.dart` fed by `hydrationProvider.loadTodaySummary` → `GET /hydration/daily/{user_id}` (`backend/api/v1/hydration.py:191-278`), which does **not** silently fall back (`raise safe_internal_error(e, "endpoint")` at :277) — and 0 ml is the correct render for an account with 0 rows. No surface persists the bootstrap value either: `preSeedFromBootstrap` (`hydration_repository.dart:439-454`) is first-paint-only and bails once real data exists, and both Home entry points (`home_screen.dart:979`, `hero_nutrition_card.dart:158`) unconditionally fire `loadTodaySummary`, overwriting it. The bare `except: pass` at 276-283 is the intended "no configured goal" branch — PostgREST `.single()` **raises** on zero rows, the normal state for a user with no `user_settings` row. The only user-visible half (fabricated 2500) is already filed as row 109. |
| 116 | MED | Nutrition → Hydration → TODAY'S LOG | Log entries render the raw **UTC** clock time instead of the user's local time, so the row contradicts the status bar on the same screen: the entry reads "21:46" while the device clock reads 4:47 PM. | `ui_nutrition_08_hydscroll2_s.png` — status bar "4:47", log row "Water · via Home · 250ml · **21:46**". Supabase `hydration_logs` row 27813f60-aa8c-4ac0-9f44-6fc202318277: `logged_at` = 2026-08-05T**21:46**:03.030389+00:00, i.e. 16:46 America/Chicago (users.timezone = America/Chicago). The UI prints the UTC hour verbatim. Aggregation itself is correct (`local_date` = 2026-08-05), so this is display-only — but it is wrong for every non-UTC user. **Proof the bug is local to this widget and not an app-wide tz gap:** the Nutrition → JOURNAL → FEED entry for the 2026-07-28 food log renders "Breakfast · 1:34 AM" (`ui_nutrition_52_jul28_s.png`), which is the correct America/Chicago rendering of that row's `logged_at` = 2026-07-28T06:34:11Z. **Code root cause (verified):** `mobile/flutter/lib/screens/nutrition/tabs/hydration_tab_part_stat_item.dart:368-369` builds the string as `'${log.loggedAt!.hour...}:${log.loggedAt!.minute...}'` with **no `.toLocal()`**; `HydrationLog.loggedAt` is produced by `DateTime.parse(json['logged_at'])` (`lib/data/models/hydration.g.dart:18`) on the PostgREST `…+00:00` string, so it is an `isUtc` DateTime and `.hour` returns the UTC hour verbatim. Every sibling surface does the guard correctly — `nutrition_journal_tab.dart:556,603-605,1153`, `logged_meals_section.dart:2759`, `daily_quest_deck.dart:31` all use `isUtc ? toLocal()`. Row #160 independently confirms the same `hydration_logs` rows render as local (4:46/4:48) in the Home timeline, so the defect is confined to this one widget. Supabase re-queried 2026-08-05: row 27813f60… → `logged_at` `2026-08-05 21:46:03.030389+00`, `local_date` `2026-08-05`, `source` `home` — exactly a 5 h (CDT) offset from the 4:47 PM status bar. **Sev HIGH→MED:** wrong displayed value, but display-only (aggregation, `local_date`, and all other time surfaces are correct) and a one-line `.toLocal()` fix; nothing is corrupted or blocked. | Nutrition → LOG WATER → +250ML → scroll to TODAY'S LOG; compare the entry time to the status-bar clock. | DONE — hydration_tab_part_stat_item.dart _LogEntry now .toLocal()s loggedAt before reading hour/minute (was rendering the raw UTC clock). |

| 128 | MED | Nutrition → DAILY → SET A TARGET → bottom action bar | The "USE RECOMMENDED" button overflows its row — Flutter reports RIGHT OVERFLOWED BY 5.2 PIXELS and the label is clipped to "USE RECOMMEND…" beside SAVE TARGETS. | `ui_nutrition_56_settarget_s.png` — hazard stripe and banner between the two buttons. Per the note below, a release build clips the label silently with no banner. **Verified from code + font metrics 2026-08-05 (not a harness artifact, not a data gap — the footer is static chrome that renders identically for every account).** Root cause is in the design-system component, not the sheet: `widgets/design_system/zealova_button.dart:39-50` builds `Row(mainAxisSize: max) → Text(label.toUpperCase(), ZType.lbl(14, letterSpacing: 2.5))` with **no `Flexible`/`Expanded`, no `overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis`, no `FittedBox`** — the `Text` therefore claims its full intrinsic width and any row narrower than that overflows. Layout chain measured end to end: `GlassSheet` is full-bleed (`glass_sheet.dart:167-208`, no horizontal margin) → `edit_targets_sheet.dart:2004` `EdgeInsets.only(left: 20, right: 20)` → 350 pt content → the pinned footer `Row` at `:2483-2535` is `[Expanded, SizedBox(10), Expanded]` → 170 pt per button → `ZealovaButton`'s own `horizontal: 20` padding (`zealova_button.dart:58`) leaves **130.0 pt** for the label. Parsing the real bundled face (`assets/fonts/BarlowCondensed-Bold.ttf`, the `weight: 700` entry in `pubspec.yaml:291-292`, upm 1000) gives "USE RECOMMENDED" an advance of **133.3 pt** at 14 px + 2.5 letter-spacing × 15 chars — 3.3 pt wider than the box before kerning/hinting, so the overflow is arithmetically unavoidable (Flutter's shaper reports 5.2). The prediction was checked against the pixels: at 390 pt logical (screenshot is 1170×2532 @3x) the chain puts the button edges at 30 / 285 / 300 / 555 px, which is exactly where they land in the frame. Persistence across sub-tabs is likewise structural, not incidental — the footer sits **outside** the tab-pane `Column` (which closes at `:2465`), so it is the same widget on all three; corroborated on `ui_nutrition_58_permeal_s.png` (PER MEAL) with the identical "RIGHT OVERFLOWED BY 5.2 PIXELS" stripe. Still present at HEAD: `zealova_button.dart` has exactly one commit (`4f3c613b`) and no overflow guard, so unlike #144 there is no already-landed fix awaiting a rebuild. Device-width dependent and worse on smaller phones — 430 pt (Pro Max) yields 150 pt avail and does **not** clip, 390 pt clips by ~3-5 pt, 375 pt (SE / 13 mini) clips by ~11 pt. Not a duplicate: #139 (2000/150/200 pre-fill) and #140 (fabricated "Maintain Weight" banner) are different defects that merely share this screenshot; #117/#144 are the coach card's `Row` in `coach_hero_card.dart`; #131 is the fasting bar. Sev **MED** upheld — no data loss, no crash, and the truncated label is still inferable, but it clips silently in release on the majority of iPhone widths and the missing guard is design-system-wide (every narrow `ZealovaButton` inherits it), which is more than a one-screen cosmetic nit. | Nutrition → DAILY → SET A TARGET → look at the sticky bottom bar. | DONE — the design-system fix already landed at the chokepoint (commit 35f7e08f, lib/widgets/design_system/zealova_button.dart): the label is now Flexible + maxLines:1 + TextOverflow.ellipsis, so no ZealovaButton anywhere can overflow its row — the row's own diagnosis (bare Text with no Flexible) is exactly what was repaired, and it fixes all 46 construction sites, not just this footer. Added the row's exact reported geometry as a case to the app-wide gate: test/ui_gates/no_overflow_gate_test.dart 'ZealovaButton pair in a pinned footer row (row 128)' — Padding(h:20) > Row([Expanded, SizedBox(10), Expanded]) with the real 'Use recommended'/'Save targets' labels, run across the 3 widths x 3 text scales matrix. Negative-tested: reverted the Flexible/ellipsis to a bare Text -> the gate recorded 'ZealovaButton pinned footer pair @ small-320 / textScale=1.0' as a layout failure; restored -> 0 failures. flutter analyze clean. |

The hero variant wraps its decorated Container in a Stack for the accent left edge; Stack's default StackFit.loose hands non-positioned children LOOSE constraints, so the visible surface shrink-wrapped to its content's intrinsic width while the Stack still filled the tight width Expanded gave it -- hence a narrower card than its outlined sibling with the watermark sliced at the edge. Changed to fit: StackFit.passthrough (forwards the incoming constraints unchanged: tight stays tight so it fills like the outlined/flat variants; loose stays loose so intrinsic sizing still works in unbounded contexts) -- not StackFit.expand, which would force tight width in loose contexts. **Standardise on this, do not invent a fourth style.** The corpus supports exactly two back controls and they are used in roughly equal numbers (≈35 vs ≈36 of ~71 full-screen routes), so there is no incumbent to defer to — pick one and apply it everywhere. The recommendation is the **circled control**: a filled dark circle containing a left arrow, pinned top-left, since it is the only one that survives floating over hero imagery and illustrations (Programs and Library exercise detail both need that) and it is already the convention in the two densest lanes, Stats & Scores and You. Title treatment should ride with it as one header component rather than being chosen per screen (see row 163). Bottom sheets: **drag handle plus an explicit close-× top-right** — the handle alone is already the ~45-of-60 majority, and adding the × costs nothing while removing the guesswork; reserve a back-chevron in a sheet header exclusively for genuinely nested sheets, where it means "up one level", never "close". Dialogs are already consistent (text buttons, no icon dismiss) — leave them alone. And the error boundary must render a real Back/Close button before anything else here is worth doing (row 205).

## Notes on this sweep

**Note on RenderFlex overflow:** Flutter paints the yellow/black hazard stripe and the "RIGHT OVERFLOWED BY N PIXELS" banner **only in debug builds** — in release the overflowing content is silently clipped with no visual warning. That is exactly why this class survives unnoticed: a laid-out-too-wide row still ships, just without the stripe telling anyone. Screenshot sweeps of debug builds are currently the only place these surface.

**⚠ Tooling defect affecting every "does not scroll" finding.** The swipe helper
used to drive these sweeps (`scratchpad/sw.sh`) issued `cliclick m:` between
`dd:` and `du:`. `m:` posts a mouse-MOVE, not a mouse-DRAG — so Flutter never
received a scroll gesture and **no screen ever scrolled**, whether or not it was
scrollable. Any finding of the form "does not scroll" / "unreachable below the
fold" produced with it is unsafe until re-driven with the fixed helper
(`sw2.sh`, which uses `dm:`). Rows marked **SUSPECT — re-verify** above are the
known affected set. A sweep agent caught this after nearly filing a false CRIT
against a sheet that scrolls perfectly well; the tell was that the Home feed —
obviously scrollable — also refused to move.

Related: `xcrun simctl io screenshot` was observed lagging several actions
behind reality for a stretch. If a control looks dead, re-capture after a pause
before calling it broken.
| 222 | HIGH | App-wide — GlassSheet (shared bottom sheet used by settings, coach chat sessions list, workout screen, stats, health/sleep, and others) | `FlutterError: ListTile background color or ink splashes may be invisible.` fired from 8 distinct Sentry issue groups (FITWIZ-FLUTTER-HE, HW, G9, H7, J2, HX, J4, HJ; ~157 combined events, ~8 distinct users, routes `/settings`, `/chat/sessions`, `/workout/:id`, `/stats`, `/health/sleep`) over 18 days. Every event's `flutter_error_details.information` carries the identical `DecoratedBox` fingerprint (`BoxDecoration(color: black@0.5/white@0.7, border: Border(top: BorderSide(white@0.15 / black@0.08, width 0.5)), borderRadius: BorderRadius.only(topLeft: 28, topRight: 28))`) — one root cause, not eight. `GlassSheet` (`lib/widgets/glass_sheet.dart`), the app's single "preferred way to show bottom sheets," renders its glass background as a `Container`/`BoxDecoration` wrapped directly around the caller's `child` with no `Material` of its own. Any screen that places a `ListTile` inside a `GlassSheet` forces that `ListTile` to reach past the decorated glass background to find a Material ancestor outside the modal route, which Flutter flags as invisible ink/background. | 8 Sentry issues (see Evidence), ~157 events / ~8 users, prod release 1.2.67+1140, iOS + Android, 18 days. | Open any GlassSheet-based bottom sheet containing a ListTile (e.g. Settings → any options sheet, Coach → chat sessions list, Workout detail sheet, Stats sheet, Health → sleep sheet). | DONE (sentry) — root-caused to `GlassSheet` (`lib/widgets/glass_sheet.dart`) wrapping `child` in a `Container`/`BoxDecoration` with no Material ancestor of its own; fixed ONCE at the chokepoint by wrapping the sheet's Column content in `Material(type: MaterialType.transparency)` immediately inside the decorated Container, so every GlassSheet caller (settings, chat sessions, workout, stats, health/sleep, and any future screen) gets a paint target for descendant ListTiles/InkWells that isn't occluded by the glass background. test/widgets/glass_sheet_listtile_test.dart added — builds a GlassSheet with a ListTile child and asserts FlutterError.onError never fires; negative-tested (reverting the Material wrap reproduces the exact Sentry assertion text, confirmed fails, restored, confirmed passes). |
| 223 | HIGH | XP Leaderboard loading skeleton (`/xp-leaderboard`, reached from You → Trophy Room and the Overview SOCIAL tile) | `FlutterError: A RenderFlex overflowed by 2.0 / 1.9 pixels on the bottom.` — two Sentry issue groups (FITWIZ-FLUTTER-J9, 16 occurrences; FITWIZ-FLUTTER-J8, 10 occurrences; same trace, prod release 1.2.67+1140, iOS, 2026-08-06). Both `debugCreator` traces bottom out in the SAME widget: `Column ← SkeletonText ← Expanded ← Row ← Padding ← DecoratedBox ← ConstrainedBox ← Container ← SkeletonCard ← Column ← Padding ← SkeletonList`. `SkeletonCard` (`lib/core/widgets/skeleton/skeleton_list.dart`) pairs an explicit `padding: EdgeInsets.all(16)` with a `Container` whose `decoration` carries `Border.all(...)`; `Container` silently ADDS the border's own `border.dimensions` on top of the caller's `padding` (`BoxDecoration.padding`), shrinking the true interior height below `height - 2*padding`. `xp_leaderboard_screen.dart` calls `SkeletonCard(height: 64, leadingSize: 40, lines: 2)` twice (the XP board and the streaks/workouts board loading states) — a 2-line `SkeletonText` needs exactly 32px and `64 - 2*16 = 32` matches with zero slack, so the ~2px border claim alone overflows it. Not `/xp-leaderboard`-specific: the same `SkeletonCard(height: ...)` shape is reused by ~20 screens app-wide (settings, goals, social `leaderboard_tab.dart`, streaks, nutrition, stats), several sharing the same razor-exact height. | FITWIZ-FLUTTER-J9 (16 events, 1 user), FITWIZ-FLUTTER-J8 (10 events, 1 user), both prod, iOS 26.1, route `/xp-leaderboard`. | You → Trophy Room → bar-chart action → XP LEADERBOARD (or Overview → SOCIAL tile) while the board is loading. | DONE (sentry) — fixed at the `SkeletonCard` chokepoint (`lib/core/widgets/skeleton/skeleton_list.dart`): `height` is now a MINIMUM (`constraints: BoxConstraints(minHeight: height)`) instead of a tight `Container` height, so the card grows to fit its content (leading circle / text lines / border inset) instead of ever clipping it — fixes the whole class for every `SkeletonCard(height: ...)` caller, not just xp-leaderboard's two call sites. `test/core/widgets/skeleton/skeleton_card_overflow_test.dart` added — pumps the exact `height:64, leadingSize:40, lines:2` shape plus a width×textScale matrix and asserts `takeException()` is null; negative-tested (reverting the `constraints` fix back to `height: height` reproduces the exact 'RenderFlex overflowed by 2.0 pixels on the bottom' Sentry text on both tests, confirmed fails, restored, confirmed passes). |

Culprit frame is `main.dart` (the global `FlutterErrorIntegration` handler, not the offending widget) and this event's `flutter_error_details` did not carry a `debugCreator` widget-ancestry chain, so the exact overflowing widget inside `WorkoutDetailScreen` could not be pinned down from Sentry data alone without deeper investigation in the file. | FITWIZ-FLUTTER-50 (37 events, 1 user), route `/workout/:id`. | 225 | LOW | App startup (`_initNonCriticalServices`, `Purchases.configure` at `/splash`) | `RevenueCat API key missing at configure` — FITWIZ-FLUTTER-1B, 394 events / 15 users over 4 months, still firing, initially triaged as CRIT ("15 real users could not subscribe"). Investigation disproved that: every one of the 15 affected `user.id`s carries `device.simulator: true` (14 iPhone iOS-Simulator installs, 1 Android emulator) — `search_events` faceted by `user.id, device.simulator` showed zero real-device rows. Root cause is `run_ios.sh` / `run_ios_debug.sh` / `run_ios_dev.sh` only exporting `REVENUECAT_APPLE_KEY` when a developer explicitly sets it, combined with `configureRevenueCat()` escalating ANY missing key to a Sentry warning-level alert with no distinction between "real device, broken release build" and "simulator, dev didn't export a local key" (simulators can never complete a real IAP regardless, so a missing key there is expected, not a bug). No evidence any real paying user was ever affected. Separately (defense-in-depth, same ownership: build config that supplies the RevenueCat key): `.github/workflows/android-release.yml` passed the Android key under the dart-define name `REVENUECAT_ANDROID_KEY`, but `environment_config.dart` reads `REVENUECAT_GOOGLE_KEY` — a latent mismatch that would have shipped a Play Store build with a dead paywall had that workflow ever been run (`gh run list` confirms it never has been). | FITWIZ-FLUTTER-1B (394 events, 15 users, all `device.simulator:true`), https://saig-n0.sentry.io/issues/FITWIZ-FLUTTER-1B | Run the app via `run_ios.sh`/`run_ios_debug.sh` without exporting `REVENUECAT_APPLE_KEY` on an iOS Simulator. | DONE (sentry) — `configureRevenueCat()` (`lib/core/providers/subscription_provider.dart`) now checks `isPhysicalDeviceForBilling()` (new `device_info_plus`-backed helper) before deciding whether a missing/placeholder key is Sentry-alert-worthy (real device — still loud, unchanged) or breadcrumb-only (simulator/emulator — expected, no longer pages). Also fixed the misnamed dart-define in `.github/workflows/android-release.yml` (`REVENUECAT_ANDROID_KEY` → `REVENUECAT_GOOGLE_KEY`) plus a fail-loud preflight so a future run can't silently ship a keyless build. `test/core/providers/subscription_provider_test.dart` added — asserts real-device missing-key alerts Sentry and simulator missing-key does not; negative-tested (reverting the device-class gate to unconditional-alert reproduces the exact failure `Expected: false, Actual: <true>`, confirmed fails, restored, confirmed passes). |
| 226 | HIGH | App startup -- coach daily-insight prefetch racing dailyCoachInsightProvider (`/splash`) | `BootstrapPrefetchService.prefetchCoachInsight` -- fired fire-and-forget from the `/splash` -> home router redirect (`app_router.dart`'s `BootstrapPrefetchService.prefetch(ref)` call) -- built its own independent `GET /coach/daily-insight` request by hand instead of going through `dailyCoachInsightProvider`, the SAME provider `CoachHeroCard`'s `ref.watch` (and `MainShell`'s prewarm waves) independently fetch from. Both check the disk cache first, but check-then-fetch has no lock between two unrelated callers, so on a genuine cold start (fresh install, or a new calendar day -- no cache entry yet) BOTH see a miss and BOTH fire. The two calls also frequently carry DIFFERENT `tz` query params -- the bootstrap prefetch fell back to `DateTime.now().timeZoneName` (a device offset abbreviation, e.g. `CDT`) whenever `timezoneProvider` was still loading, while `dailyCoachInsightProvider` refuses to fetch at all until the real IANA zone resolves, then fetches with e.g. `America/Chicago` -- so `ApiClient`'s existing in-flight GET coalescing (keyed on the full query string) never even saw them as the same request. Two concurrent requests to a Gemini-backed endpoint that itself can take the full 30s receive-timeout window doubles the odds either one times out, and shows up as a duplicate-shaped call to Sentry's N+1 detector. A live trace (`8170d486074443adb81f012e7aabe670`) captured this exact pattern at app startup: ~50 endpoints fired within about 1 second of a warm start, several duplicated (progress/xp x3, nutrition/summary/daily x3, fasting endpoints x2 each) -- daily-insight's duplicate was one instance of that broader startup-storm class, which is out of this lane's scope (backend/other-lane concurrency) beyond the one endpoint this lane owns. | FITWIZ-FLUTTER-AX (36 events, 6 users, DioException receive-timeout at 30s, route=/splash, first seen 2026-06-05, still firing) and FITWIZ-FLUTTER-FY (8 events, 4 users, "N+1 API Call" on `/coach/daily-insight?date=*&source=*&tz=*`, route=/splash, first seen 2026-07-03, still firing). | Cold-start the app (fresh install, or first launch of a new calendar day) with no `cache_coach_insight` disk entry yet, while the backend is slow to answer `/coach/daily-insight`. | DONE (sentry) -- `BootstrapPrefetchService.prefetchCoachInsight` (`mobile/flutter/lib/data/services/bootstrap_prefetch_service.dart`) now reads through `ref.read(dailyCoachInsightProvider.future)` instead of building its own request, so Riverpod's single-Future-per-provider-instance semantics dedupe every caller onto ONE network request regardless of tz-resolution timing; the provider still does everything the old code did by hand (disk-cache-first, write-through cache, `ref.keepAlive()` on success). `test/services/bootstrap_prefetch_coach_insight_dedup_test.dart` added -- reproduces the exact production race (prefetch fires while `timezoneProvider.isLoading`, `dailyCoachInsightProvider` reads moments later once it resolves) and asserts exactly one `/coach/daily-insight` request; negative-tested (reverting to the old independent-fetch implementation reproduces 2 requests with mismatched `tz` values -- `Expected: <1> Actual: <2>` -- confirmed fails, restored, confirmed passes). `flutter analyze` clean on both touched files. |
| 227 | CRIT | Workout complete screen (`/workout-complete`) | `AssertionError: 'package:flutter/src/rendering/object.dart': Failed assertion: line 5713 pos 12: 'node.built': is not true.` thrown from `_RenderObjectSemantics.debugCheckForBuilds` during `PipelineOwner.flushSemantics`, inside a scheduler callback -- the same widget-lifecycle-violation family as the `_dependents.isEmpty` crash fixed today in commit 019c5f02 (row 8: a controller/animation disposed while a scheduled callback from a still-mounted descendant -- there, an autofocused TextField's caret-scroll callback -- fires against an already-detached render tree, corrupting the next frame's semantics/element pass and taking down the WHOLE screen, not just the offending widget). 982 occurrences in a single ~3-minute window (`02:54:36`-`02:57:52`) from one user is a tight failure loop consistent with that same pattern: something on this screen re-enters/rebuilds every frame after the tree is already torn down, so the assertion refires continuously instead of failing once. `WorkoutCompleteScreen` (`lib/screens/workout/workout_complete_screen.dart`) owns a `ConfettiController` (disposed in `dispose()`) and renders a confetti overlay/celebration on the completion screen -- a plausible source of a callback that keeps scheduling itself post-unmount, but this is a hypothesis for the owning lane to verify against the full widget tree, not a confirmed root cause (out of this lane's ownership: `lib/screens/workout/*`, not `lib/screens/splash/*`). | FITWIZ-FLUTTER-GZ (982 events, 1 user, fatal, substatus=escalating, route=/workout-complete, first/last seen 2026-08-01 within a 3-minute span). | Complete a workout and land on the post-workout summary/celebration screen. | OPEN (sentry) -- root cause not fixed; file is owned by another lane (`lib/screens/workout/workout_complete_screen.dart`), reported per this lane's ownership boundary rather than edited. Diagnostic starting point: check every `AnimationController`/`ConfettiController`/dialog/overlay on this screen for a callback (post-frame, animation-status listener, timer) that fires without a `mounted` guard after the route pops -- the exact class of bug row 8's fix addressed (owning the controller's lifecycle via the widget's own `initState`/`dispose` instead of a caller-managed teardown). |
| 228 | HIGH | Nutrition log confirmation Hero/SnackBar (`/trends/custom`) | `FlutterError: There are multiple heroes that share the same tag within a subtree.` -- the colliding tag is literally the logged-food text (`<SnackBar Hero tag - Text("Logged Scrambled Eggs (with Butter) + Toast (Whole Wheat with Butter)")>`), meaning the Hero tag is built from dynamic, non-unique content (a food-log description) instead of a stable per-item identifier -- two SnackBars/Heroes carrying the same logged-item text in the same subtree collide. Thrown from `HeroController._maybeStartHeroTransition` during a drawn frame, so this is a route-transition-time crash risk, not merely a lint. | FITWIZ-FLUTTER-HR (5 events, 1 user, route=/trends/custom, first/last seen 2026-08-05 within ~23 minutes). | Log two similar/identical food items in quick succession (or navigate while a food-logged SnackBar/Hero transition is in flight) on the custom trends screen. | OPEN (sentry) -- not fixed; the culprit screen (`/trends/custom`, a stats/nutrition surface) is outside this lane's ownership (`lib/main.dart`, `lib/app.dart`, `lib/screens/splash/*`), reported rather than edited. Fix is to key the Hero tag on a stable unique id (log entry id / timestamp), never on the rendered label text -- grep for `Hero(tag:` combined with a SnackBar or food-log description on the trends/nutrition surfaces to find the exact call site. |

## Coverage

**Read this as a whitelist.** A surface listed under *Surfaces reached* was
driven on device this pass. **A surface that is not listed was not looked at —
that is not a statement that it works.** The *NOT reached* lists below are each
lane's own record of what it skipped and why.

**Lanes driven:** Stats · Settings · Exercise Library · Programs · Nutrition · Home · Coach · You.

**Lanes NOT driven:** Workouts (Workout tab proper, active session, set logging,
rest timer, workout detail, workout summary, post-workout share) · Onboarding,
sign-up and sign-in · Paywall and purchase.
